It also pins `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) end-to-end via `CliRunner` so any drift on the exit-code contract surfaces here first.\n\n## Test matrix\n\n| Slice | TEST-004 AC bullet | Test class | Cases |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Missing claude HARD | \"missing claude fails HARD\" | `TestMissingClaudeHard` | 4 |\n| `--no-mcp` SOFT-SKIP | \"`--no-mcp` soft-skips MCP evals\" | `TestNoMcpSoftSkip` | 4 |\n| XFAIL classification | \"XFAIL classification supported\" | `TestXfailClassification` | 4 |\n| Doctor status rendering | \"doctor output renders correct status string per classification\" | `TestDoctorClassificationRendering` | 7 |\n| Coverage pin | (meta) | `test_test_004_slice_coverage_is_complete` | 1 |\n| **Total** | | | **20** |\n\n### Slice 1 — `TestMissingClaudeHard` (4 cases)\n\n- `test_missing_claude_lands_in_hard_failures` — `CapabilityGates.check_all()` with `shutil.which(\"claude\")` returning `None` populates `binary.claude` in `hard_failures`; `soft_skips` and `soft_xfails` stay empty.\n- `test_missing_claude_row_carries_hard_failure_mode` — same setup; assert the `CapabilityStatus` row carries `failure_mode=\"hard\"`, `passed=False`, `skipped_by_flag=False`, and a \"not found\"-bearing detail.\n- `test_doctor_exits_two_when_claude_missing` — end-to-end via `CliRunner`: doctor exits `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) and the HARD-failure artifact lands on stderr naming `binary.claude`.\n- `test_doctor_json_reports_hard_failure_on_missing_claude` — same end-to-end check via `--json`; assert the payload's `hard_failures` includes `binary.claude` and `soft_skips` does not.\n\n### Slice 2 — `TestNoMcpSoftSkip` (4 cases)\n\n- `test_no_mcp_flag_skips_mcp_servers_even_when_probe_passes` — `mcp_probe` injects a passing probe; assert every MCP row lands in `soft_skips`, `skipped_by_flag=True`, and `passed=False` (override semantics).\n- `test_no_mcp_flag_skips_mcp_servers_when_probe_fails` — MCP binaries absent; HARD binaries present; assert HARD floor stays green and every default MCP row routes through `soft_skips` with `skip_flags=(\"--no-mcp\",)`.\n- `test_no_mcp_flag_does_not_affect_hard_rows` — assert no HARD row gains `skipped_by_flag=True` when the flag is active (HARD descriptors do not declare a `skip_flag`).\n- `test_doctor_no_mcp_flag_lands_mcp_rows_in_soft_skips` — end-to-end via `CliRunner` `--json --no-mcp`: assert `payload[\"skip_flags\"] == [\"--no-mcp\"]` and every default MCP row appears in `soft_skips`.\n\n### Slice 3 — `TestXfailClassification` (4 cases)\n\n- `test_failing_xfail_capability_lands_in_soft_xfails` — inject a custom `_CapabilitySpec(failure_mode=\"xfail\")` via the `capabilities=` constructor hook with the probe target absent from PATH; assert `soft_xfails` contains the row and the HARD / SOFT-SKIP buckets stay empty.\n- `test_xfail_row_carries_xfail_failure_mode_when_failing` — same setup; assert the `CapabilityStatus` row's `failure_mode=\"xfail\"`, `passed=False`, `skipped_by_flag=False`.\n- `test_passing_xfail_capability_does_not_land_in_failure_buckets` — flip the spec to target a binary that resolves; assert all three failure buckets stay empty and the row's `passed=True`.\n- `test_xfail_status_serialises_through_capability_report` — build a `CapabilityReport` carrying an xfail row and assert `to_json()` produces a JSON-serialisable payload with `soft_xfails` containing the row name and `failure_mode=\"xfail\"` preserved.\n\n### Slice 4 — `TestDoctorClassificationRendering` (7 cases)\n\n- `test_renders_hard_failure_marker` — pin `[XX] <description> (HARD)` for failing HARD rows.\n- `test_renders_soft_skip_probe_failure_marker` — pin `[--] <description> (SOFT-SKIP)` for probe-failed SOFT-SKIP rows.\n- `test_renders_skipped_by_flag_marker_when_no_mcp_active` — pin `[--] <description> (skipped by flag)` and `skip flags: --no-mcp`. Negative: `(SOFT-SKIP)` MUST NOT appear when the override marker is present.\n- `test_renders_xfail_marker` — pin `[??] <description> (xfail)`.\n- `test_renders_passing_row_with_ok_marker` — pin `[ok] <description>` with NO trailing tag.\n- `test_hard_failure_artifact_names_offending_capability` — `render_hard_failure_artifact` lists the name + description + detail for each HARD failure.\n- `test_doctor_cli_emits_distinct_strings_for_each_classification` — end-to-end via `CliRunner`: HARD floor green; MCP probes absent; assert `(SOFT-SKIP)` appears in the default run. Re-invoke with `--no-mcp`; assert `(skipped by flag)` appears AND `skip flags: --no-mcp` appears.\n\n### Coverage pin — `test_test_004_slice_coverage_is_complete`\n\nA meta-test that asserts each TEST-004 AC bullet has at least one corresponding test class in this module. Future drift in the AC list will break this test, forcing a coordinated spec + test update. Mirrors the meta-test in D-0040 (TEST-002) and D-0041 (TEST-003).\n\n## Classification matrix (the contract this module pins)\n\n| `failure_mode` | Bucket on miss          | Doctor marker     | Trailing tag           | Override flag | Aborts run? |\n|---------------|-------------------------|-------------------|------------------------|---------------|-------------|\n| `hard`        | `hard_failures`         | `[XX]`            | ` (HARD)`              | — none —      | Yes (`exit 2`) |\n| `skip`        | `soft_skips`            | `[--]`            | ` (SOFT-SKIP)`         | `--no-mcp` (default roster) | No |\n| `skip` (flag-forced) | `soft_skips`     | `[--]`            | ` (skipped by flag)`   | `--no-mcp`    | No |\n| `xfail`       | `soft_xfails`           | `[??]`            | ` (xfail)`             | — none in default roster — | No |\n| (passing)     | — none —                | `[ok]`            | — none —               | n/a           | No |\n\nThe matrix maps 1:1 to the `_row_marker()` helper in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py` and the bucket-routing logic in `CapabilityGates.check_all()`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n| AC | Source | Verified by The natural way to test xfail support is to monkeypatch `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` to inject an extra row. **D-0042 deliberately does not do this.**\n\nThe `CapabilityGates` constructor already exposes a `capabilities=` keyword precisely for this purpose (\"Tests inject a custom spec tuple via the `capabilities` keyword to mock PATH state without monkeypatching `shutil`,\" per `capabilities.py:233-234`). Using the documented hook keeps the test honest about how a production xfail entry would land — through the same API surface — and prevents accidental coupling to a private implementation detail. If the default roster ever grows an xfail entry, this slice continues to pin the classification surface unchanged.\n\n## Why pin the exact doctor marker strings instead of just checking the failure-bucket routing\n\n`CapabilityGates.check_all()` routes failing rows into `hard_failures` / `soft_skips` / `soft_xfails` — testing that routing alone proves the bucket logic works. But the operator-facing contract is the **rendered checklist line** that a human reads in CI output:\n\n```\n  [XX] Claude CLI on PATH (HARD) -- not found on PATH\n  [--] Auggie MCP server reachable (SOFT-SKIP) -- MCP server binary not on PATH\n  [--] Auggie MCP server reachable (skipped by flag) -- /opt/auggie\n  [??] Optional xfail probe (xfail) -- not found on PATH\n  [ok] Claude CLI on PATH -- /usr/bin/claude\n```\n\nEach glyph + tag pair is a distinct visual signal. If `_row_marker()` ever drifted (e.g. someone changed `[XX]` to `[!!]` to \"make it look prettier\"), the bucket-routing tests would still pass but operators would silently lose the visual disambiguation. The marker-string assertions catch that class of regression directly.\n\n## Why test the `--no-mcp` override against BOTH passing and failing probes\n\nA naïve `--no-mcp` test only covers one branch: probe fails AND flag is active → row in `soft_skips`. But the *override semantics* the design-spec promises is \"force soft-skip regardless of probe result.\" Two tests are therefore needed:\n\n1. Probe passes (via injected `mcp_probe`) + flag active → row STILL in `soft_skips` with `skipped_by_flag=True`. Proves the flag overrides a green probe.\n2. Probe fails (binary absent) + flag active → row in `soft_skips`. Proves the flag's behaviour is consistent when the probe agrees.\n\nIf `--no-mcp` ever regressed to \"skip only when the probe also fails,\" test (1) would fail. If it regressed to \"ignore the probe and always set `skipped_by_flag=True` regardless of probe result, even when probe passes\", the `passed=False` assertion in test (1) would still hold because the override forces `passed=False` — but the `skipped_by_flag=True` assertion would catch any drift to \"ignore the flag entirely.\"\n\n## Why pin `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` as `== 2` only transitively (via `CliRunner.exit_code`)\n\n`test_doctor.py` already imports and references `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` directly. D-0042 imports the same constant and uses it in `CliRunner.exit_code` assertions — the constant's value (2) is pinned by `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` in `commands.py`, not by a literal `== 2` here. This keeps the spec-level \"exit 2 on HARD failure\" contract enforced exactly once (in `test_doctor.py::test_cli_doctor_exits_two_when_hard_capability_missing` via direct integer comparison there); D-0042's role is to pin the *classification-to-exit-code* mapping, not the integer value.\n\nIf a future change reroutes HARD failures through a different exit-code constant, both this module and `test_doctor.py` will fail in the same PR, making the regression obvious. Pinning the literal `2` in two places would create a maintenance burden without adding signal.\n\n## Why the doctor-end-to-end test re-invokes with `--no-mcp` instead of asserting both markers in one run\n\nThe default doctor run already includes a `vendored.ptytest` SOFT-SKIP row (M2-deferred per `_check_ptytest_vendored`), so `(SOFT-SKIP)` will appear in the output regardless of MCP probe results. To prove that `(skipped by flag)` *specifically* renders when `--no-mcp` is active, the test re-invokes with the flag and asserts the override marker appears AND the `skip flags: --no-mcp` line appears. This isolates the two markers cleanly across two runs rather than relying on which row happened to use which marker in a combined run.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c95452c2-0167-4cf7-a7e3-7000bf8f8732","uuid":"4fde89ce-9bbd-4ce3-974f-12940e441ff7","timestamp":"2026-05-20T10:37:52.427Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"pytest-capability-family.log\npytest-T02.23.log\n---\n# D-0042 — Evidence\n\n**Task**: T02.23 (Phase 2 — cliEval harness)\n**Deliverable**: TEST-004 capability gate tests (R-042)\n**Module**: `tests/cli/eval/test_capability_classifications.py`\n\n## Verification command (T02.23 Steps[5])\n\n```\nuv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_capability_classifications.py -v\n```\n\n## Result\n\n**20 passed in 0.14s** — every classification slice green; To prove that `(skipped by flag)` *specifically* renders when `--no-mcp` is active, the test re-invokes with the flag and asserts the override marker appears AND the `skip flags: --no-mcp` line appears. This isolates the two markers cleanly across two runs rather than relying on which row happened to use which marker in a combined run.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

``EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`` (plus the optional CLI-supplied\n    ``output_dir`` for that invocation).\n\n    Attributes:\n        path: The original, un-resolved candidate path (rendered    )\n\n\ndef _resolve_prefix(prefix: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve an allowlist entry to its absolute, symlink-free form.\n\n    ``EvalConfig`` ships relative defaults (``.dev/eval-runs``) so the\n    repo-local prefix anchors against the process CWD. ``strict=False``\n    keeps the helper usable in tests / fresh checkouts where the scratch\n    directory may not yet exist.\n    \"\"\"\n\n None,\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve ``path`` and verify it lives under an AC12 allowed root.\n\n    The helper is the single ingress point for the AC12 allowlist policy\n    (see roadmap R-016 / FR-ISO2). Callers that mint scratch directories,\n    per-eval HOMEs, or output dirs MUST route the target through this\n    function *before* any filesystem write so a malformed path cannot\n    leak side effects onto disk.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Candidate scratch path. ``str`` accepted for ergonomics;\n            normalised to ``Path``. The value is expanded (``~``) and\n            resolved (``strict=False``) so symlinks and relative segments\n            do not escape the allowlist.\n        config: Optional :class:`EvalConfig` whose\n            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` provides the policy allowlist. Falls\n            back to a default-constructed instance, which yields the\n            canonical pair ``(/tmp/eval-runs, .dev/eval-runs)``.\n            Passing the project's live ``EvalConfig`` guarantees there is\n            exactly one source of truth — no other module embeds a copy.\n        output_dir: Optional CLI-supplied ``--output-dir`` to extend the\n            allowlist for this call only. The value is resolved the same\n            way as the rest of the allowlist; passing it does NOT mutate\n            ``config``.\n\n    Returns:\n        The resolved absolute :class:`~pathlib.Path` for ``path``. Callers\n        SHOULD use this returned value rather than the original input when\n        composing further sub-paths so subsequent comparisons stay stable.\n\n    Raises:\n        ScratchRootViolation: ``path`` (after resolution) is not equal to\n            and not relative to any entry in the resolved allowlist. The\n            exception carries the original input, the resolved form, and\n            the resolved allowlist so reporters can render verbatim\n            forensics. CLI callers MUST map it to\n            :data:`SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    config = config if config is not None else EvalConfig()\n\n    # Build the allowlist from EvalConfig (sole source of truth) plus the\n    # optional per-call --output-dir. Resolving each prefix once here keeps\n    # the comparison loop below allocation-free.\n    allowed: list[Path] = [\n        _resolve_prefix(prefix) for prefix in config.allowed_scratch_roots\n    ]\n    if output_dir is not None:\n        allowed.append(_resolve_prefix(Path(output_dir)))\n\n    candidate = Path(path)\n    resolved = candidate.expanduser().resolve(strict=False)\n\n    for prefix in allowed:\n        # ``is_relative_to`` catches strict sub-paths; Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - probe is a user-supplied callable\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"claude.min_version\",\n            passed=False,\n           name=\"claude.min_version\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=\"claude --version not callable\",\n tuple[CapabilityStatus, ...],\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Return a new ``CapabilityReport`` with ``extras`` appended and\n    failure-bucket tallies updated.\n\n    The base report's hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n        soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n        soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n    )\n\n\ndef build_doctor_report(\n    skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = dict(spec.isolation)\n    if spec.inputs:\n        payload[\"inputs\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.inputs]\n    if spec.expects:\n        payload[\"expects\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.expects]\n    if spec.parameterize:\n        payload[\"parameterize\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.parameterize]\n    return payload\n\n\ndef _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed: ParsedSuite) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project a :class:`ParsedSuite` into the manifest envelope shape.\n\n    ``evals`` is the post-parameterize-expansion list (the canonical\n    \"what would run if you invoked ``eval run``\" view match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_012sQPoXje647SNH2itLh1m3","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_013U1tFZveY2bbwUdnZASgMo","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/docs/eval/scratch-roots.md","content":"# cliEval scratch root policy (OPS-002 / AC12)\n\n**Roadmap:** R-043 / OPS-002 (cross-module scratch-root enforcement).\n**Source of truth:** `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` in\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/config.py` (T01.01 / D-0001). Every CLI command,\nisolation primitive, and reporter that touches a scratch directory funnels\nthrough `resolve_scratch_root()` (T01.19 / D-0016) — no module embeds a\nsecond copy of the allowlist.\n\n## The 3 allowed roots\n\nThe cliEval harness creates per-eval scratch HOMEs, per-eval working trees,\nand any operator-supplied `--output-dir` under **exactly one** of the\nfollowing roots. Anything else is rejected before any filesystem write.\n\n| # | Allowed root                  | Provenance                                                                                                                                                            |\n|---|-------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| 1 | `/tmp/eval-runs/`             | Canonical M1 scratch root. Lives in `_default_allowed_scratch_roots()` so a fresh checkout works without operator configuration.                                      |\n| 2 | `<repo>/.dev/eval-runs/`      | Repo-relative scratch root for sessions that want their artifacts on the same filesystem as the source tree. Anchors against process CWD via `Path.resolve()`.        |\n| 3 | `--output-dir <path>`         | Operator-supplied path passed on the command line. Extends the allowlist **for the current invocation only**; never mutates `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`.       |\n\nThe canonical policy text emitted by `superclaude eval doctor` and every\nother CLI boundary is the `SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY` constant in\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/config.py` — that constant and this document\nmust list the same three roots. Drift between them is a release blocker\ncaught by `tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_policy.py`.\n\n## Why an allowlist (and not a denylist)\n\nThe cliEval harness runs the real `claude` binary against per-eval HOMEs\nthat may contain operator-controlled hooks, settings, and prompt material.\nA misconfigured scratch root that resolves under `~/.claude/`,\n`/etc/`, `/var/lib/`, or any other location with non-eval state would let\na buggy or malicious suite mutate the operator's real environment. The\nallowlist closes that surface by **defaulting to refusal**: only the\nthree named roots above (resolved symlink-free via `Path.resolve()`) are\never accepted.\n\nThe same allowlist powers four layered defenses:\n\n1. **Loader-time validation** (`SuiteLoader`, T01.07) rejects suites\n   whose declared scratch root resolves outside the allowlist.\n2. **Doctor pre-flight** (`superclaude eval doctor --output-dir`) tells\n   operators *before* running a suite whether their `--output-dir` will\n   be accepted, and quotes the policy verbatim when it will not.\n3. **HomeIsolation containment** (`containment_guard`, T02.08) re-applies\n   the check after `mkdtemp` so a symlink swap between loader-time and\n   setup-time is still caught.\n4. **Atomic setup wrapper** (T02.13) preserves the partial HOME with a\n   `setup_failed` artifact tag when any of the above refuse, so\n   forensics survive even when the harness aborts mid-setup.\n\nSee `D-0016/spec.md`, `D-0029/spec.md`, and `D-0043/spec.md` for the\ndeliverable-level details.\n\n## How operators encounter the policy\n\n### `superclaude eval doctor --output-dir <path>`\n\n```\n$ superclaude eval doctor --output-dir /etc/foo\neval doctor: scratch path escapes AC12 allowlist: path=/etc/foo resolved=/etc/foo allowed=[/tmp/eval-runs, /config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/eval-runs]\n\ncliEval scratch root policy (AC12 / OPS-002):\n  Scratch HOMEs, per-eval working trees, and --output-dir targets MUST\n  resolve under one of these allowed roots:\n    1. /tmp/eval-runs/        -- canonical M1 scratch root\n    2. <repo>/.dev/eval-runs/ -- repo-relative scratch root\n    3. --output-dir <path>    -- extends the allowlist for the current\n                                  invocation only (call-scoped, never\n                                  mutates EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots)\n  Anything else is rejected before any filesystem write.\n  Authoritative reference: docs/eval/scratch-roots.md.\n$ echo $?\n2\n```\n\nThe doctor exits `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` (= 2), matching the\nloader-error trio so CI scripts only need to recognize a single\n\"harness refused to operate before any filesystem write\" outcome.\n\n### `superclaude eval run` (M3+)\n\nFuture `eval run`, `eval gather`, and any other command that accepts an\noperator-supplied path will catch `ScratchRootViolation` the same way\nand render it through `format_scratch_root_violation()`. New CLI surfaces\nMUST funnel through that helper instead of reimplementing the message;\nthis is the OPS-002 cross-module consistency guarantee.\n\n### Programmatic callers\n\nCallers inside `src/superclaude/cli/eval/` invoke\n`resolve_scratch_root(path, config=config, output_dir=output_dir)`\ndirectly. The helper accepts an optional `output_dir` keyword that\nextends the allowlist for the call only (see\n`test_output_dir_is_call_scoped_not_persistent` in\n`tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_allowlist.py`).\n\n## Updating the policy\n\nAny change to the allowed roots **must** land in three places in one\ncommit:\n\n1. `_default_allowed_scratch_roots()` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/config.py`.\n2. `SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY` in the same module.\n3. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_013U1tFZveY2bbwUdnZASgMo","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /config/workspace/IronClaude/docs/eval/scratch-roots.md (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c95452c2-0167-4cf7-a7e3-7000bf8f8732","uuid":"9e544058-a746-4ba8-9c17-5ae4051b371e","timestamp":"2026-05-20T10:40:53.264Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/docs/eval/scratch-roots.md","content":"# cliEval scratch root policy (OPS-002 / AC12)\n\n**Roadmap:** R-043 / OPS-002 (cross-module scratch-root enforcement).\n**Source of truth:** `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots` in\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/config.py` (T01.01 / D-0001). Every CLI command,\nisolation primitive, and reporter that touches a scratch directory funnels\nthrough `resolve_scratch_root()` (T01.19 / D-0016) — no module embeds a\nsecond copy of the allowlist.\n\n## The 3 allowed roots\n\nThe cliEval harness creates per-eval scratch HOMEs, per-eval working trees,\nand any operator-supplied `--output-dir` under **exactly one** of the\nfollowing roots. Anything else is rejected before any filesystem write.\n\n| # | Allowed root                  | Provenance                                                                                                                                                            |\n|---|-------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| 1 | `/tmp/eval-runs/`             | Canonical M1 scratch root. Lives in `_default_allowed_scratch_roots()` so a fresh checkout works without operator configuration.                                      |\n| 2 | `<repo>/.dev/eval-runs/`      | Repo-relative scratch root for sessions that want their artifacts on the same filesystem as the source tree. Anchors against process CWD via `Path.resolve()`.        |\n| 3 | `--output-dir <path>`         | Operator-supplied path passed on the command line. Extends the allowlist **for the current invocation only**; never mutates `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`.       |\n\nThe canonical policy text emitted by `superclaude eval doctor` and every\nother CLI boundary is the `SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY` constant in\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/config.py` — that constant and this document\nmust list the same three roots. Drift between them is a release blocker\ncaught by `tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_policy.py`.\n\n## Why an allowlist (and not a denylist)\n\nThe cliEval harness runs the real `claude` binary against per-eval HOMEs\nthat may contain operator-controlled hooks, settings, and prompt material.\nA misconfigured scratch root that resolves under `~/.claude/`,\n`/etc/`, `/var/lib/`, or any other location with non-eval state would let\na buggy or malicious suite mutate the operator's real environment. The\nallowlist closes that surface by **defaulting to refusal**: only the\nthree named roots above (resolved symlink-free via `Path.resolve()`) are\never accepted.\n\nThe same allowlist powers four layered defenses:\n\n1. **Loader-time validation** (`SuiteLoader`, T01.07) rejects suites\n   whose declared scratch root resolves outside the allowlist.\n2. **Doctor pre-flight** (`superclaude eval doctor --output-dir`) tells\n   operators *before* running a suite whether their `--output-dir` will\n   be accepted, and quotes the policy verbatim when it will not.\n3. **HomeIsolation containment** (`containment_guard`, T02.08) re-applies\n   the check after `mkdtemp` so a symlink swap between loader-time and\n   setup-time is still caught.\n4. **Atomic setup wrapper** (T02.13) preserves the partial HOME with a\n   `setup_failed` artifact tag when any of the above refuse, so\n   forensics survive even when the harness aborts mid-setup.\n\nSee `D-0016/spec.md`, `D-0029/spec.md`, and `D-0043/spec.md` for the\ndeliverable-level details.\n\n## How operators encounter the policy\n\n### `superclaude eval doctor --output-dir <path>`\n\n```\n$ superclaude eval doctor --output-dir /etc/foo\neval doctor: scratch path escapes AC12 allowlist: path=/etc/foo resolved=/etc/foo allowed=[/tmp/eval-runs, /config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/eval-runs]\n\ncliEval scratch root policy (AC12 / OPS-002):\n  Scratch HOMEs, per-eval working trees, and --output-dir targets MUST\n  resolve under one of these allowed roots:\n    1. /tmp/eval-runs/        -- canonical M1 scratch root\n    2. <repo>/.dev/eval-runs/ -- repo-relative scratch root\n    3. --output-dir <path>    -- extends the allowlist for the current\n                                  invocation only (call-scoped, never\n                                  mutates EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots)\n  Anything else is rejected before any filesystem write.\n  Authoritative reference: docs/eval/scratch-roots.md.\n$ echo $?\n2\n```\n\nThe doctor exits `SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` (= 2), matching the\nloader-error trio so CI scripts only need to recognize a single\n\"harness refused to operate before any filesystem write\" outcome.\n\n### `superclaude eval run` (M3+)\n\nFuture `eval run`, `eval gather`, and any other command that accepts an\noperator-supplied path will catch `ScratchRootViolation` the same way\nand render it through `format_scratch_root_violation()`. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012PqXxyAANQw9QFxd9s4EvU","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0042 — Design notes\n2\t\n3\t## Why inject an xfail spec via `capabilities=` instead of monkeypatching the default roster\n4\t\n5\tThe default `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` table has no `xfail` rows today — every default spec is either `hard` or `skip`. The natural way to test xfail support is to monkeypatch `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` to inject an extra row. **D-0042 deliberately does not do this.**\n6\t\n7\tThe `CapabilityGates` constructor already exposes a `capabilities=` keyword precisely for this purpose (\"Tests inject a custom spec tuple via the `capabilities` keyword to mock PATH state without monkeypatching `shutil`,\" per `capabilities.py:233-234`). Using the documented hook keeps the test honest about how a production xfail entry would land — through the same API surface — and prevents accidental coupling to a private implementation detail. If the default roster ever grows an xfail entry, this slice continues to pin the classification surface unchanged.\n8\t\n9\t## Why pin the exact doctor marker strings instead of just checking the failure-bucket routing\n10\t\n11\t`CapabilityGates.check_all()` routes failing rows into `hard_failures` / `soft_skips` / `soft_xfails` — testing that routing alone proves the bucket logic works. But the operator-facing contract is the **rendered checklist line** that a human reads in CI output:\n12\t\n13\t```\n14\t  [XX] Claude CLI on PATH (HARD) -- not found on PATH\n15\t  [--] Auggie MCP server reachable (SOFT-SKIP) -- MCP server binary not on PATH\n16\t  [--] Auggie MCP server reachable (skipped by flag) -- /opt/auggie\n17\t  [??] Optional xfail probe (xfail) -- not found on PATH\n18\t  [ok] Claude CLI on PATH -- /usr/bin/claude\n19\t```\n20\t\n21\tEach glyph + tag pair is a distinct visual signal. If `_row_marker()` ever drifted (e.g. someone changed `[XX]` to `[!!]` to \"make it look prettier\"), the bucket-routing tests would still pass but operators would silently lose the visual disambiguation. The marker-string assertions catch that class of regression directly.\n22\t\n23\t## Why test the `--no-mcp` override against BOTH passing and failing probes\n24\t\n25\tA naïve `--no-mcp` test only covers one branch: probe fails AND flag is active → row in `soft_skips`. But the *override semantics* the design-spec promises is \"force soft-skip regardless of probe result.\" Two tests are therefore needed:\n26\t\n27\t1. Probe passes (via injected `mcp_probe`) + flag active → row STILL in `soft_skips` with `skipped_by_flag=True`. Proves the flag overrides a green probe.\n28\t2. Probe fails (binary absent) + flag active → row in `soft_skips`. Proves the flag's behaviour is consistent when the probe agrees.\n29\t\n30\tIf `--no-mcp` ever regressed to \"skip only when the probe also fails,\" test (1) would fail. If it regressed to \"ignore the probe and always set `skipped_by_flag=True` regardless of probe result, even when probe passes\", the `passed=False` assertion in test (1) would still hold because the override forces `passed=False` — but the `skipped_by_flag=True` assertion would catch any drift to \"ignore the flag entirely.\"\n31\t\n32\t## Why pin `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` as `== 2` only transitively (via `CliRunner.exit_code`)\n33\t\n34\t`test_doctor.py` already imports and references `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` directly. D-0042 imports the same constant and uses it in `CliRunner.exit_code` assertions — the constant's value (2) is pinned by `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` in `commands.py`, not by a literal `== 2` here. This keeps the spec-level \"exit 2 on HARD failure\" contract enforced exactly once (in `test_doctor.py::test_cli_doctor_exits_two_when_hard_capability_missing` via direct integer comparison there); D-0042's role is to pin the *classification-to-exit-code* mapping, not the integer value.\n35\t\n36\tIf a future change reroutes HARD failures through a different exit-code constant, both this module and `test_doctor.py` will fail in the same PR, making the regression obvious. Pinning the literal `2` in two places would create a maintenance burden without adding signal.\n37\t\n38\t## Why the doctor-end-to-end test re-invokes with `--no-mcp` instead of asserting both markers in one run\n39\t\n40\tThe default doctor run already includes a `vendored.ptytest` SOFT-SKIP row (M2-deferred per `_check_ptytest_vendored`), so `(SOFT-SKIP)` will appear in the output regardless of MCP probe results. To prove that `(skipped by flag)` *specifically* renders when `--no-mcp` is active, the test re-invokes with the flag and asserts the override marker appears AND the `skip flags: --no-mcp` line appears. This isolates the two markers cleanly across two runs rather than relying on which row happened to use which marker in a combined run.\n41\t\n42\t## Why a coverage-pin meta-test\n43\t\n44\t`test_test_004_slice_coverage_is_complete` walks the AC bullet list and asserts each has a corresponding test class in the module. This is overkill for a 4-class module — but the same pattern is used in D-0040 (4 test classes) and D-0041 (4 test classes), and uniformity matters more than minimal cost. The cost is one assertion; the value is forcing future AC additions to land with their tests in the same PR.\n45\t\n46\t## Sibling regression\n47\t\n48\tAfter landing this module, the five-module capability family (`test_capability_classifications.py` + `test_capability_gates.py` + `test_capability_dataclass.py` + `test_capability_report.py` + `test_doctor.py`) runs clean at **92 passed in 0.22s**. No drift in sibling deliverables.\n49\t\n50\t## Verification method\n51\t\n52\tT02.23 is STANDARD tier with `Verification Method: Direct test execution` and `Sub-Agent Delegation: None`. Per phase-2-tasklist: \"Run `uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_capability_classifications.py -v`.\" Evidence log captures the 20-passed result.\n53\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c95452c2-0167-4cf7-a7e3-7000bf8f8732","uuid":"506343ca-b649-47a5-86d0-2bdfb326f8b5","timestamp":"2026-05-20T10:45:12.475Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0042/notes.md","content":"# D-0042 — Design notes\n\n## Why inject an xfail spec via `capabilities=` instead of monkeypatching the default roster\n\nThe default `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` table has no `xfail` rows today — every default spec is either `hard` or `skip`. The marker-string assertions catch that class of regression directly.\n\n## Why test the `--no-mcp` override against BOTH passing and failing probes\n\nA naïve `--no-mcp` test only covers one branch: probe fails AND flag is active → row in `soft_skips`. Proves the flag's behaviour is consistent when the probe agrees.\n\nIf `--no-mcp` ever regressed to \"skip only when the probe also fails,\" test (1) would fail. If it regressed to \"ignore the probe and always set `skipped_by_flag=True` regardless of probe result, even when probe passes\", the `passed=False` assertion in test (1) would still hold because the override forces `passed=False` — but the `skipped_by_flag=True` assertion would catch any drift to \"ignore the flag entirely.\"\n\n## Why pin `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` as `== 2` only transitively (via `CliRunner.exit_code`)\n\n`test_doctor.py` already imports and references `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` directly. D-0042 imports the same constant and uses it in `CliRunner.exit_code` assertions — the constant's value (2) is pinned by `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` in `commands.py`, not by a literal `== 2` here. This keeps the spec-level \"exit 2 on HARD failure\" contract enforced exactly once (in `test_doctor.py::test_cli_doctor_exits_two_when_hard_capability_missing` via direct integer comparison there); D-0042's role is to pin the *classification-to-exit-code* mapping, not the integer value.\n\nIf a future change reroutes HARD failures through a different exit-code constant, both this module and `test_doctor.py` will fail in the same PR, making the regression obvious. Pinning the literal `2` in two places would create a maintenance burden without adding signal.\n\n## Why the doctor-end-to-end test re-invokes with `--no-mcp` instead of asserting both markers in one run\n\nThe default doctor run already includes a `vendored.ptytest` SOFT-SKIP row (M2-deferred per `_check_ptytest_vendored`), so `(SOFT-SKIP)` will appear in the output regardless of MCP probe results. To prove that `(skipped by flag)` *specifically* renders when `--no-mcp` is active, the test re-invokes with the flag and asserts the override marker appears AND the `skip flags: --no-mcp` line appears. This isolates the two markers cleanly across two runs rather than relying on which row happened to use which marker in a combined run.\n\n## Why a coverage-pin meta-test\n\n`test_test_004_slice_coverage_is_complete` walks the AC bullet list and asserts each has a corresponding test class in the module. This is overkill for a 4-class module — but the same pattern is used in D-0040 (4 test classes) and D-0041 (4 test classes), and uniformity matters more than minimal cost. The cost is one assertion; the value is forcing future AC additions to land with their tests in the same PR.\n\n## Sibling regression\n\nAfter landing this module, the five-module capability family (`test_capability_classifications.py` + `test_capability_gates.py` + `test_capability_dataclass.py` + `test_capability_report.py` + `test_doctor.py`) runs clean at **92 passed in 0.22s**. No drift in sibling deliverables.\n\n## Verification method\n\nT02.23 is STANDARD tier with `Verification Method: Direct test execution` and `Sub-Agent Delegation: None`. Per phase-2-tasklist: \"Run `uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_capability_classifications.py -v`.\" Evidence log captures the 20-passed result.\n","numLines":53,"startLine":1,"totalLines":53}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01QbJpMPiiexiSmMaNucsARc","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Sn7i9qZdqWHH5zjAzd2eAH","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0043/notes.md","content":"# D-0043 — Design notes\n\n## Why a `SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY` constant and a `format_scratch_root_violation()` renderer rather than inline strings at each catch site\n\nThe temptation at every CLI catch site is to write:\n\n```python\nexcept ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n    click.echo(f\"eval doctor: {exc}\\nAllowed roots: /tmp/eval-runs/, ...\", err=True)\n    sys.exit(2)\n```\n\nThat works for one catch site but creates an N-way drift surface as\nsoon as `eval run`, `eval gather`, and any future operator-facing\ncommand lands. Each one rewrites the policy paragraph from memory;\neach one rots independently when the allowlist changes. OPS-002's\njob is to **prevent** that drift — so the policy lives in one constant\n(`SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY`) and one renderer (`format_scratch_root_violation`),\nand every CLI surface is required to funnel through them. The renderer\nalso accepts the exception object directly, so the per-violation\nforensic detail (offending path, resolved form, allowlist checked)\nstays distinct from the static policy text.\n\n`tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_policy.py::test_renderer_uses_blank_line_separator`\npins the separator format so a future \"simplification\" that\nconcatenates the two strings without a blank line would fail visibly.\n\n## Why `--output-dir` validation runs **before** HARD-capability probing\n\nThe doctor command's natural flow is: probe HARD capabilities → render\nchecklist → exit. Inserting `--output-dir` validation **before** the\nprobe loop is a deliberate ordering choice for two reasons:\n\n1. **Operator clarity.** A misconfigured `--output-dir` is an\n   \"invocation refused\" outcome, not a \"your environment is broken\"\n   outcome. Running the HARD probes first would mix unrelated\n   capability failures (missing `claude` binary, etc.) into the\n   doctor's output and bury the actual problem.\n2. **Side-effect avoidance.** HARD probes may touch the filesystem\n   (the `claude --version` invocation reads the binary, capability\n   checks may inspect HOME-shaped paths). If the operator's\n   `--output-dir` is hostile (e.g. `/etc/foo`), we want to refuse\n   **before any filesystem read** that might depend on the rejected\n   path. `test_scratch_violation_takes_precedence_over_hard_probe`\n   pins this ordering: the test injects a deliberately broken\n   `claude` PATH and asserts that the scratch-root rejection still\n   wins.\n\nThe exit code (`SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE = 2`) is the same as\n`HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE = 2` — both signify \"harness refused to operate.\"\nThat collision is intentional: CI scripts only need to recognize one\n\"refused\" exit code, and the stderr payload disambiguates which class\nof refusal fired.\n\n## Why three doc-anti-drift tests (not one big assertion)\n\n`test_scratch_roots_doc_exists`, `test_scratch_roots_doc_names_three_allowed_roots`,\nand `test_scratch_roots_doc_references_runtime_modules` could have been\nfolded into one mega-test. They are split for diagnostic granularity:\n\n| Test | What a failure means |\n|---|---|\n| `_exists` | Someone deleted or renamed `docs/eval/scratch-roots.md`. The fix is to restore the file at that path. |\n| `_names_three_allowed_roots` | The doc and the policy constant disagree on the 3 roots. The fix is to update whichever drifted. |\n| `_references_runtime_modules` | The doc no longer cross-references the load-bearing module symbols. The fix is to add the missing reference so prose remains navigable to code. |\n\nA single combined test would surface as \"doc is wrong\" and force the\ndeveloper to bisect which surface drifted. Three named tests turn the\npytest failure list itself into a diagnostic.\n\n## Why `test_narrowing_config_changes_what_resolve_accepts` proves single-source-of-truth and `test_doctor_uses_default_evalconfig_allowlist` proves doctor wiring (two tests, not one)\n\nThe first iteration of this slice tried to use monkeypatch against\n`_default_allowed_scratch_roots()` to narrow the doctor's effective\nallowlist, then assert the doctor rejected what the unpatched allowlist\nwould have accepted. **It didn't work** — `EvalConfig` uses\n`field(default_factory=_default_allowed_scratch_roots)`, and Python\ncaptures the function reference at class-definition time, not on every\nconstruction. Monkeypatching the module attribute changed what\n*future* `EvalConfig` classes would see if they were re-defined, but\nnot what `EvalConfig()` resolved to in the current process.\n\nThe fix split the intent into two tests:\n\n* `test_narrowing_config_changes_what_resolve_accepts` — passes an\n  explicitly narrowed `EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(Path(\"/x\"),))`\n  directly to `resolve_scratch_root`, proving that the resolver\n  respects whatever allowlist it receives. This is the\n  \"single-source-of-truth\" claim.\n* `test_doctor_uses_default_evalconfig_allowlist` — calls the doctor\n  twice with two different `--output-dir` values (one in the default\n  allowlist, one outside it) in the same fixture, asserting the\n  doctor's accept/reject pair matches the default-`EvalConfig`\n  allowlist. This is the \"doctor wires to default config\" claim.\n\nTogether they pin the contract the original mega-test was trying to\npin, without the frozen-dataclass footgun.\n\n## Why the policy doc lives in `docs/eval/`, not in the deliverable artifacts directory\n\n`.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0043/spec.md` is a\n*deliverable-tracking* document. It lives under `.dev/` because it is\nphase-scoped: once the release lands and `current/` rotates, the spec\nbecomes a historical record. The **policy document** itself is part\nof the project's operator-facing surface — it must be checked into\n`docs/eval/` so it ships with the package and survives release\nrotation. The deliverable spec cross-references the doc; the doc\ncross-references the runtime modules and the deliverable spec. Two\nlocations, two purposes, one policy.\n\n## Sibling-regression posture\n\nBefore landing, the family (`test_doctor.py`, `test_config.py`,\n`test_scratch_root_allowlist.py`, `test_scratch_root_policy.py`)\nruns at **69 passed in 0.18s** — no drift in adjacent deliverables\n(D-0029 HomeIsolation, D-0016 `resolve_scratch_root` direct tests,\nD-0001 `EvalConfig` field validation). The new `--output-dir` Click\noption does not change the doctor's default exit code (`test_doctor.py`\nstill asserts exit 0 for green environments), and the renderer does\nnot change `str(ScratchRootViolation)` (the original \"AC12\" assertion\nin `test_scratch_root_allowlist.py` still holds).\n\n## Verification method\n\nT02.25 is STRICT tier with `Verification Method: Direct test execution\n+ sub-agent quality-engineer review`. Per phase-2-tasklist Step 5: \"Run\n`uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_policy.py -v`.\"\nEvidence log captures the 16-passed result. Sub-agent\nquality-engineer review is the next item on the T02.25 checklist\n(STRICT-tier policy: cross-module changes require an independent\nadversarial sweep). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Te6FfVUCwW1BY9NzYjHN8p","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0043 — Design notes\n2\t\n3\t## Why a `SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY` constant and a `format_scratch_root_violation()` renderer rather than inline strings at each catch site\n4\t\n5\tThe temptation at every CLI catch site is to write:\n6\t\n7\t```python\n8\texcept ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n9\t    click.echo(f\"eval doctor: {exc}\\nAllowed roots: /tmp/eval-runs/, ...\", err=True)\n10\t    sys.exit(2)\n11\t```\n12\t\n13\tThat works for one catch site but creates an N-way drift surface as\n14\tsoon as `eval run`, `eval gather`, and any future operator-facing\n15\tcommand lands. Each one rewrites the policy paragraph from memory;\n16\teach one rots independently when the allowlist changes. OPS-002's\n17\tjob is to **prevent** that drift — so the policy lives in one constant\n18\t(`SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY`) and one renderer (`format_scratch_root_violation`),\n19\tand every CLI surface is required to funnel through them. The renderer\n20\talso accepts the exception object directly, so the per-violation\n21\tforensic detail (offending path, resolved form, allowlist checked)\n22\tstays distinct from the static policy text.\n23\t\n24\t`tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_policy.py::test_renderer_uses_blank_line_separator`\n25\tpins the separator format so a future \"simplification\" that\n26\tconcatenates the two strings without a blank line would fail visibly.\n27\t\n28\t## Why `--output-dir` validation runs **before** HARD-capability probing\n29\t\n30\tThe doctor command's natural flow is: probe HARD capabilities → render\n31\tchecklist → exit. Inserting `--output-dir` validation **before** the\n32\tprobe loop is a deliberate ordering choice for two reasons:\n33\t\n34\t1. **Operator clarity.** A misconfigured `--output-dir` is an\n35\t   \"invocation refused\" outcome, not a \"your environment is broken\"\n36\t   outcome. Running the HARD probes first would mix unrelated\n37\t   capability failures (missing `claude` binary, etc.) into the\n38\t   doctor's output and bury the actual problem.\n39\t2. **Side-effect avoidance.** HARD probes may touch the filesystem\n40\t   (the `claude --version` invocation reads the binary, capability\n41\t   checks may inspect HOME-shaped paths). If the operator's\n42\t   `--output-dir` is hostile (e.g. `/etc/foo`), we want to refuse\n43\t   **before any filesystem read** that might depend on the rejected\n44\t   path. `test_scratch_violation_takes_precedence_over_hard_probe`\n45\t   pins this ordering: the test injects a deliberately broken\n46\t   `claude` PATH and asserts that the scratch-root rejection still\n47\t   wins.\n48\t\n49\tThe exit code (`SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE = 2`) is the same as\n50\t`HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE = 2` — both signify \"harness refused to operate.\"\n51\tThat collision is intentional: CI scripts only need to recognize one\n52\t\"refused\" exit code, and the stderr payload disambiguates which class\n53\tof refusal fired.\n54\t\n55\t## Why three doc-anti-drift tests (not one big assertion)\n56\t\n57\t`test_scratch_roots_doc_exists`, `test_scratch_roots_doc_names_three_allowed_roots`,\n58\tand `test_scratch_roots_doc_references_runtime_modules` could have been\n59\tfolded into one mega-test. They are split for diagnostic granularity:\n60\t\n61\t| Test | What a failure means |\n62\t|---|---|\n63\t| `_exists` | Someone deleted or renamed `docs/eval/scratch-roots.md`. The fix is to restore the file at that path. |\n64\t| `_names_three_allowed_roots` | The doc and the policy constant disagree on the 3 roots. The fix is to update whichever drifted. |\n65\t| `_references_runtime_modules` | The doc no longer cross-references the load-bearing module symbols. The fix is to add the missing reference so prose remains navigable to code. |\n66\t\n67\tA single combined test would surface as \"doc is wrong\" and force the\n68\tdeveloper to bisect which surface drifted. Three named tests turn the\n69\tpytest failure list itself into a diagnostic.\n70\t\n71\t## Why `test_narrowing_config_changes_what_resolve_accepts` proves single-source-of-truth and `test_doctor_uses_default_evalconfig_allowlist` proves doctor wiring (two tests, not one)\n72\t\n73\tThe first iteration of this slice tried to use monkeypatch against\n74\t`_default_allowed_scratch_roots()` to narrow the doctor's effective\n75\tallowlist, then assert the doctor rejected what the unpatched allowlist\n76\twould have accepted. **It didn't work** — `EvalConfig` uses\n77\t`field(default_factory=_default_allowed_scratch_roots)`, and Python\n78\tcaptures the function reference at class-definition time, not on every\n79\tconstruction. Monkeypatching the module attribute changed what\n80\t*future* `EvalConfig` classes would see if they were re-defined, but\n81\tnot what `EvalConfig()` resolved to in the current process.\n82\t\n83\tThe fix split the intent into two tests:\n84\t\n85\t* `test_narrowing_config_changes_what_resolve_accepts` — passes an\n86\t  explicitly narrowed `EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(Path(\"/x\"),))`\n87\t  directly to `resolve_scratch_root`, proving that the resolver\n88\t  respects whatever allowlist it receives. This is the\n89\t  \"single-source-of-truth\" claim.\n90\t* `test_doctor_uses_default_evalconfig_allowlist` — calls the doctor\n91\t  twice with two different `--output-dir` values (one in the default\n92\t  allowlist, one outside it) in the same fixture, asserting the\n93\t  doctor's accept/reject pair matches the default-`EvalConfig`\n94\t  allowlist. This is the \"doctor wires to default config\" claim.\n95\t\n96\tTogether they pin the contract the original mega-test was trying to\n97\tpin, without the frozen-dataclass footgun.\n98\t\n99\t## Why the policy doc lives in `docs/eval/`, not in the deliverable artifacts directory\n100\t\n101\t`.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0043/spec.md` is a\n102\t*deliverable-tracking* document. It lives under `.dev/` because it is\n103\tphase-scoped: once the release lands and `current/` rotates, the spec\n104\tbecomes a historical record. The **policy document** itself is part\n105\tof the project's operator-facing surface — it must be checked into\n106\t`docs/eval/` so it ships with the package and survives release\n107\trotation. The deliverable spec cross-references the doc; the doc\n108\tcross-references the runtime modules and the deliverable spec. Two\n109\tlocations, two purposes, one policy.\n110\t\n111\t## Sibling-regression posture\n112\t\n113\tBefore landing, the family (`test_doctor.py`, `test_config.py`,\n114\t`test_scratch_root_allowlist.py`, `test_scratch_root_policy.py`)\n115\truns at **69 passed in 0.18s** — no drift in adjacent deliverables\n116\t(D-0029 HomeIsolation, D-0016 `resolve_scratch_root` direct tests,\n117\tD-0001 `EvalConfig` field validation). Every CLI command,\n6\tisolation primitive, and reporter that touches a scratch directory funnels\n7\tthrough `resolve_scratch_root()` (T01.19 / D-0016) — no module embeds a\n8\tsecond copy of the allowlist.\n9\t\n10\t## The 3 allowed roots\n11\t\n12\tThe cliEval harness creates per-eval scratch HOMEs, per-eval working trees,\n13\tand any operator-supplied `--output-dir` under **exactly one** of the\n14\tfollowing roots. Anything else is rejected before any filesystem write.\n15\t\n16\t| # | Allowed root                  | Provenance                                                                                                                                                            |\n17\t|---|-------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n18\t| 1 | `/tmp/eval-runs/`             | Canonical M1 scratch root. The\n35\tallowlist closes that surface by **defaulting to refusal**: only the\n36\tthree named roots above (resolved symlink-free via `Path.resolve()`) are\n37\tever accepted.\n38\t\n39\tThe same allowlist powers four layered defenses:\n40\t\n41\t1. **Loader-time validation** (`SuiteLoader`, T01.07) rejects suites\n42\t   whose declared scratch root resolves outside the allowlist.\n43\t2. **Doctor pre-flight** (`superclaude eval doctor --output-dir`) tells\n44\t   operators *before* running a suite whether their `--output-dir` will\n45\t   be accepted, and quotes the policy verbatim when it will not.\n46\t3. **HomeIsolation containment** (`containment_guard`, T02.08) re-applies\n47\t   the check after `mkdtemp` so a symlink swap between loader-time and\n48\t   setup-time is still caught.\n49\t4. **Atomic setup wrapper** (T02.13) preserves the partial HOME with a\n50\t   `setup_failed` artifact tag when any of the above refuse, so\n51\t   forensics survive even when the harness aborts mid-setup.\n52\t\n53\tSee `D-0016/spec.md`, `D-0029/spec.md`, and `D-0043/spec.md` for the\n54\tdeliverable-level details.\n55\t\n56\t## How operators encounter the policy\n57\t\n58\t### `superclaude eval doctor --output-dir <path>`\n59\t\n60\t```\n61\t$ superclaude eval doctor --output-dir /etc/foo\n62\teval doctor: scratch path escapes AC12 allowlist: path=/etc/foo resolved=/etc/foo allowed=[/tmp/eval-runs, /config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/eval-runs]\n63\t\n64\tcliEval scratch root policy (AC12 / OPS-002):\n65\t  Scratch HOMEs, per-eval working trees, and --output-dir targets --output-dir <path>    -- extends the allowlist for the current\n70\t                                  invocation only (call-scoped, never\n71\t                                  mutates EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots)\n72\t  Anything else is rejected before any filesystem write.\n73\t  Authoritative reference: docs/eval/scratch-roots.md.\n74\t$ echo
